```
def corr_plan(n):
   L=1<<int(np.ceil(np.log2(2*n-1)))
   Fones = np.fft.rfft(np.ones(n), L)
   idx = np.r_[:n][::-1]
```

```
def lag_gradient(idx,h,n):
 k=idx-(n-1); grad=np.zeros(n)
 if k>=0:
   if k==0: grad[:] = 1-2*h
   else: grad[k:] += 1-h[:-k] if k else? careful n-k slices; grad[:n-k] -= h[k:]
 else:
   s=-k
   if s==0: ...
   else: grad[:n-s] += 1-h[s:]; grad[s:] -= h[:n-s]
 return grad
```

Gradient:

```
def _smooth_gradient(x):
    c_num = compute_c_num...
    c = c_num*dx
    z = c/tau
    maxz = z.max()
    e = np.exp(z-maxz)
    p = e/e.sum()  # weights sum1
    grad = full_gradient(x, n, p, dx)
    return grad
```

```
def full_gradient(h, n, p, scale=1.0):
    L = next_power... maybe precompute? For B,T2 use np.fft.rfft each call.
    # A = sliding sum: use np.convolve(p, np.ones(n), 'valid')? p length M. But p changes. Could use cumulative sum: cs=np.concatenate([0, np.cumsum(p)]); A[0:]=cs[n:2n]-cs[0:n]? Since A_i=sum p[i:i+n]. csum length M+1; A = csum[n:n+n]-csum[0:n].
    # B,T2: use FFT with p and p_rev. Or use np.convolve direct. For n=400, direct may be okay. SLSQP many evals; FFT maybe.
    Fp = rfft(p, L)
    Fp_rev = rfft(p[::-1], L)
    Fh = rfft(h, L)
    B = irfft(Fh*Fp, L)[n-1:2*n-1]
    T = irfft(Fh*Fp_rev, L)[n-1:2*n-1]
    return scale*(A-B-T)
```

Sparse gradient function:
```
def sparse_lags_gradient(h,n,idxs,weights):
 grad=np.zeros(n)
 for idx,w in zip(idxs,weights):
   k=idx-(n-1)
   if w==0: continue
   if k>=0:
    if k==0: grad += w*(1-2h)
    else:
      grad[k:] += w*(1-h[:n-k])
      grad[:n-k] -= w*h[k:]
   else:
    s=-k
    if s==0...
    else:
      grad[:n-s] += w*(1-h[s:])
      grad[s:] -= w*h[:n-s]
 return grad
```
The weights could be softmax over top K after normalizing. The sum of top K weights <1; scaling? For logsumexp, if we set p_top normalized to sum1 (but ignoring mass outside), gradient is not exact; but it emphasizes top lags. Could just use weights p (not renorm) so sum<1. If p_top mass small, step small. Renormalize to make updates meaningful. Use top K normalized, effectively approximate max over top K. Good.

For ADAM, if top K only, it's minimizing approximate max of active lags. Good.

Let's implement `active_indices_weights(c, top_k, temp)`:

```
cmax=c.max()
idxs=np.argsort(c)[-top_k:] (descending)
top_vals=c[idxs]
z=top_vals/temp - zmax
w=np.exp(z); w/=w.sum()
return idxs,w
```
But if top vals vary, weights concentrated. Need maybe choose top_k larger based on plateau: include all lags with c > cmax - 10*temp? But top fixed.

Projection function: Need exact sum target and box. Use bisection on shift after clipping:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
 target=n/2
 h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 for ita in range(60):
   s=h.sum(); diff=target-s
   if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
   if diff>0:
      room=1-h; sroom=room.sum()
      if sroom<1e-15: break
      h += diff/sroom*room
   else:
      if h.sum()<1e-15: break
      h += diff/h.sum()*h
 return np.clip(h,0,1)
```
This multiplicative proportional method may be slow to converge but okay. More robust: bisection on add `tau` after clipping:
Find τ such that sum(clip(h - τ*?)) target? For general projection onto hyperplane plus box, can solve by t: z_i=clip(h_i + t). Since shift only, sum is monotone in t. But h may have sum far; t range [-1,1] enough? If h sum target but violation, adding constant may work if within feasible? If feasible target always between 0 and n, and box, there is a t such that sum clip(h+t)=target if h can be shifted to feasible? If h arbitrary bounded [0,1]? We can first clip; sum in [0,n]. target n/2. There exists t? h clipped to [0,1]; as t goes from -1 to 1, clip sum from 0 to n monotonically; target in range, yes. So simple bisection on t:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
 h=np.asarray(h,float); target=n/2
 lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
 # but if h has values narrow, t may need >1? h in [0,1], yes -1 makes all 0, 1 all1.
 for _ in range(80):
   mid=(lo+hi)/2
   s=np.clip(h+mid).sum()
   if s<target: lo=mid
   else: hi=mid
 return np.clip(h+(lo+hi)/2)
```
This is exact Euclidean? Actually projection onto slice sum fixed with box is h_i + constant clipped? Wait projection onto intersection of box and hyperplane sum=target is not simply adding same constant to all h? For Euclidean projection onto affine hyperplane and box, it is clip(h + λ) with same λ? Yes projecting onto {x: sum x = b, 0≤x≤1} is obtained by simple thresholding/shift because objective separable and equality only; solve KKT x_i = clip(y_i + λ) for common λ. Since minimization ||x-y||^2 subject sum=x, yes common shift. Good. Our initial h maybe not [0,1] after updates; clipping after update? We can project using bisection on `y` not clipped first.
```
def project_feasible(y,n):
 target=n/2
 lo=-np.max(y); hi=1.0-np.min(y)
 for 50:
  mid=(lo+hi)/2
  if np.clip(y+mid).sum() < target: lo=mid
```

```
def correlation_c(h,n,L,Fones):
 hrev = h[::-1]
 Fh = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
 Fv = Fones - np.fft.rfft(hrev, L)
 c = np.fft.irfft(Fh * Fv, L)[:2*n-1]
 return c
```

D update function:

```
def update_D_for(h_change_index, delta, D, n):
    rlo = max(0, idx - n + 1) # idx-n+1 always <=0? idx in 0..n-1, idx-n+1 <=0 for idx<n-1? if idx=0 -> -n+1, so max0.
    rhi = min(n-1, idx)
    # For each r in [0, idx] actually since idx<n, n-idx+? rlo=0 always? idx-n+1 <= -1 for idx<=n-2; for idx=n-1 rlo=0. So rows 0..idx.
    for r in range(0, idx+1):
       idx1 = r - idx + n - 1  # first: r - i + n -1, i=idx
       idx2 = idx - r + n - 1
       D[r, idx1] -= delta
       if idx1 != idx2: D[r, idx2] -= delta
       else: # already subtract twice? if same, we can do -=2delta once
```

D initialization:
```
def build_D(h,n):
 M=2*n-1
 D=np.zeros((n,M))
 first = 1 - h[::-1]
 # For r, first slice: D[r, r:r+n] += first
 # Need do in loop; n=400. Could vectorize with np.add.at? Loop fine.
 for r in range(n):
    D[r, r:r+n] += first
    D[r, n-1-r : 2*n-1-r] -= h
 return D
```

```
def local_pair_search(h,n,L,Fones,deadline,max_sweeps=20,top_count=16,fracs=None):
 dx=2/n; cnum=corr_c(...)
 C=cnum.max()*dx
 D=build_D(h,n)
 # fracs compact: [1e-4,1e-3,5e-3,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0]
 for sweep in range(max_sweeps):
   if time.time()>deadline or C<=target? no target.
   # recompute D and c maybe every 3 sweeps
   D=build_D(h,n) # maybe okay
   cnum=corr_c(h,n,L,Fones).real
   C=cnum.max()*dx
   M=2n-1
   top_idx=np.argsort(cnum)[-top_count:]
   cT=cnum[top_idx]
   Dtop=D[:,top_idx]
   pos=np.full(M,-1,int); pos[top_idx]=np.arange(top_count)
   improved=False
   for i in range(n):
     hi_i? h_i = h[i]
     for j in range(n):
       if i==j: continue
       hi = min(hi_i, 1-h[j])
       if hi < 1e-12: continue
       aT = Dtop[i] - Dtop[j]
       # quick filter? If cT.max() times? We'll compute candidates.
       idx1 = n-1 + i - j
       p1 = pos[idx1]
       best_e=0; best_m=C# use cnum max? We need top max baseline `cTmax = cT.max()` (close C)
       # Maybe baseline top max = cT.max(); But C may be from lag not in top? top includes max, so cTmax=C/dx.
       base_m = cTmax
       for f in fracs:
          e=f*hi
          if e <= 0: continue
          # If aT all positive baseline? skip? compute
          vals = cT + e*aT
          m = vals.max()
          if p1>=0:
             v = vals[p1] + e*e
             if v>m: m=v
          if m < base_m - 1e-12:
             base_m=m; best_e=e
       if best_e > 0:
          e=best_e; e2=e*e
          Afull = D[i]-D[j]  # only for best; full length
          newc = cnum + e*Afull
          newc[idx1] += e2
          idx2 = n-1 + j - i
          newc[idx2] -= e2
          newC = newc.max()*dx
          if newC < C - 1e-14:
             h[i] -= e; h[j] += e
             cnum = newc
             C = newC
             # update D incrementally (or rebuild next sweep)
             update_D(D,i,-e,n); update_D(D,j,e,n)
             # Dtop rows become stale; if we continue after update, Dtop for affected rows outdated.
             # Easiest: if improved, break and restart sweep after rebuilding? That kills speed.
             # Could update Dtop rows for r affected by i,j: Dtop[r] = D[r, top_idx] for r in 0..i and 0..j? Because D rows affected for coordinate i rows 0..i, for j rows 0..j. So update those rows in Dtop.
             for r in range(0, i+1): Dtop[r] = D[r, top_idx]
             for r in range(0, j+1): Dtop[r] = D[r, top_idx]
             improved=True
   if not improved: break
```

Now continuous ADAM. Let's design robust `adam_optimize(h,n,L,Fones,deadline,tau,lr=0.01,top_k=16)`:
```
def adam_optimize(h,n,L,Fones,deadline,tau,lr=0.05,max_stall=1000):
 dx=2.0/n
 best_h=h.copy(); best_c=cmaxdx(corr_c(...))
 m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); b1=0.9;b2=0.999; t=0;lr_base=lr
 for it in range(...):
   if time>deadline: break
   t+=1
   cnum=corr_c(h,n,L,Fones).real
   c=cnum*dx
   cur=c.max()
   if cur<best_c-1e-15: best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy(); stall=0; lr=lr_base*min(1,1+0.01?); else stall+=1
   if stall>stall_limit: break or reset?
   idxs,weights = top_active(c, 16, tau)
   g = sparse_lags_gradient(h,n,idxs,weights)*dx
   m = b1*m+(1-b1)*g; v=b2*v+(1-b2)*g*g
   mh=m/(1-b1**t); vh=v/(1-b2**t)
   step=lr_base/(np.sqrt(vh)+1e-8) * mh  # elementwise? Adam: per coord lr/(sqrt(v)+eps)
   # line search:
   accepted=False
   for scale in [1,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05]:
      cand = project_feasible(h - scale*step, n)
      ccheck=corr_c(cand,n,L,Fones).real.max()*dx
      if ccheck < cur + 1e-15:
         h=cand; accepted=True; break
   if not accepted:
      # decay lr and maybe gradient-direction? If no improve, break or reduce.
      lr_base *= 0.5
      if lr_base < 1e-8: break
```
But elementwise lr vector: `step = mh/(sqrt(vh)+eps)`. If we scale, okay. Use `lr_base` scalar.
Using top active weights normalized; for non-improving, reduce. Need maybe Nesterov momentum? Fine.
However full top active gradient with normalized weights may not match objective if top lags not all. Still descends approximate max. Could be effective.
But SLSQP may be better. We can use ADAM for broad improvement after initial, then SLSQP.

Potential problem: Project after each step can undo gradient direction due box; but okay.

```
def _smooth_obj(x):
   c = corr_full(x, OBJ_N, OBJ_L, OBJ_FONES).real * (2.0/OBJ_N)
   z = c / OBJ_TAU
   zmax = z.max()
   if not np.isfinite(zmax): return 1e100
   sm = z - zmax
   # if all exp under? sum
   return float((zmax + np.log(np.exp(sm).sum())) / OBJ_TAU)
```

```
def _smooth_grad(x):
   c = corr_full(x, OBJ_N, OBJ_L, OBJ_FONES).real * (2.0/OBJ_N)
   z = c/OBJ_TAU; zmax=z.max(); sm=z-zmax
   e=np.exp(sm); p=e/e.sum()
   p = np.nan_to_num(p)
   if not p.any(): p = np.ones(len(c))/len(c)
   g = full_gradient(x, OBJ_N, p, 2.0/OBJ_N)
   return g
```

```
def normalize_sum(h,n):
 target=0.5*n
 h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 for _ in range(10):
   diff=target-h.sum()
   if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
   if diff>0:
      idx=np.argmax(1-h)
   else:
      idx=np.argmin(h)
   h[idx]=np.clip(h[idx]+diff,0,1)
 return h
```
If all at bounds impossible; feasible. This exacts within eps.

`next_pow2`:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()
```

Projection bisection:
```
def project_feasible(y,n):
    y=np.asarray(y,dtype=float)
    target=0.5*n
    lo=-np.max(y)-1e-12
    hi=1.0-np.min(y)+1e-12
    for _ in range(70):
        mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
        s=np.clip(y+mid).sum()
        if s < target: lo=mid
        else: hi=mid
    return np.clip(y+0.5*(lo+hi))
```
If y large, lo/hi fine. This is projection onto hyperplane with Euclidean. But is it always feasible? target between 0,n and y finite; yes.

`corr_full`:
```
def corr_full(h,n,L,Fones):
    hrev = h[::-1]
    Fh = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    Fv = Fones - np.fft.rfft(hrev, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(Fh * Fv, L)
    return np.real(c[:2*n-1])
```
`compute_c5(h,n,L,Fones)` maybe:
```
def compute_c5_vals(h,n,L,Fones):
    return float(np.max(corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)) * (2.0/n))
```
Need Fones create:
```
def make_fft_plan(n):
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    Fones=np.fft.rfft(np.ones(n), L)
    return L,Fones
```

`lag_gradient_one` as derived. Ensure no closures.
```
def lag_gradient_one(idx,h,n):
    k=idx-(n-1)
    g=np.zeros(n)
    if k >= 0:
       if k==0: g[:] = 1.0 - 2.0*h
       else:
          g[k:] += 1.0 - h[:n-k]
          g[:n-k] -= h[k:]
    else:
       s=-k
       if s==0: g[:] = 1.0 - 2.0*h
       else:
          g[:n-s] += 1.0 - h[s:]
          g[s:] -= h[:n-s]
    return g
```
Note for k>0: g[k:] length n-k, h[:n-k]. For k<0: g[:n-s] length n-s; h[s:] length n-s; g[s:] length n-s; h[:n-s] length n-s. Good.
`sparse_gradient` loops:
```
def sparse_gradient(h,n,idxs,weights):
   g=np.zeros(n)
   for idx,w in zip(idxs,weights):
      if abs(w)<1e-14: continue
      g += w * lag_gradient_one(idx,h,n)
   return g
```
This allocates per idx; could optimize in loop to avoid intermediate:
```
for ...:
  k=...
  if k>=0: g[k:] += w*(1-h[:...]); g[:...] -= w*h[...]
```
Implement for speed.

`top_active`:
```
def top_active(c, top_k, tau):
    M=len(c)
    top_k=min(top_k,M)
    idxs=np.argsort(c)[-top_k:]
    vals=c[idxs]
    zmax=np.max(vals)/tau
    # To avoid overflow, z=vals/tau-zmax
    z=vals/tau - zmax
    # If vals maybe large? okay.
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=e.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
       w=np.zeros(top_k); w[np.argmax(vals)]=1.0
    else:
       w=e/s
    return idxs,w
```
If tau very small, vals/tau can overflow before shift. Use stable: `zmax = np.max(vals) / tau`; if zmax > 700? vals/tau may overflow for max? In Python dividing large by tiny could inf. Better compute z = (vals - vmax)/tau. `vmax = vals.max(); z=(vals - vmax)/tau`; exp <=1. Do that.

`adam_optimize` details:
```
def adam_optimize(h,n,L,Fones,deadline,tau=0.01,top_k=16,lr=0.05,max_iters=20000,stall_limit=2000):
    dx=2.0/n
    cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)
    best_c=float(cnum.max()*dx); best_h=h.copy()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    stall=0; lr_cur=lr
    b1=0.9; b2=0.999
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       t +=1
       cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)
       c=cnum*dx
       cur=float(c.max())
       if cur < best_c - 1e-15:
          best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy(); stall=0
          # maybe increase lr slightly
       else:
          stall +=1
       if stall > stall_limit or lr_cur < 1e-7: break
       idxs,w = top_active(c, top_k, tau)
       g = sparse_gradient(h,n,idxs,w) * dx
       m = b1*m + (1-b1)*g
       v = b2*v + (1-b2)*(g*g)
       mh = m/(1.0 - b1**t)
       vh = v/(1.0 - b2**t)
       step = mh/(np.sqrt(vh)+1e-8)
       accepted=False
       for scale in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02):
           cand = project_feasible(h - lr_cur*scale*step, n)
           ccheck_corr = corr_full(cand,n,L,Fones)
           ccheck = float(ccheck_corr.max()*dx)
           if ccheck < cur + 1e-14:
               h = cand
               accepted=True
               break
       if not accepted:
           lr_cur *= 0.5
           # optional perturb
    return best_h,best_c
```
But if no improvement after scale, h unchanged; next iteration same c -> stall may exceed. We can add random perturb if no accept:
```
if not accepted:
   if lr_cur<1e-6:
      # random small perturb
      rs=np.random.RandomState(it)
      htmp=project_feasible(h+0.01*rs.randn(n),n)
      if c(h_tmp)<cur: h=h_tmp
      lr_cur=lr*0.5
   else: lr_cur*=0.5
```
But use global rng? We can pass `rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)`? Top-level no? Use `np.random.RandomState(it)`.
`b1**t` for large t underflows to 0; fine but computing each iter maybe. Could maintain bias correction with scalar.

Maybe SLSQP better; ADAM could take time. We'll allocate time.

`build_D` and `update_D` as above. Need `M=2*n-1`.
`prepare_top_data`:
```
def prepare_top_data(cnum,D,n,top_count):
    M=2*n-1
    if top_count > M: top_count=M
    top_idx = np.argsort(cnum)[-top_count:]
    cT = cnum[top_idx].copy()
    Dtop = D[:, top_idx].copy()
    pos = np.full(M, -1, dtype=np.int32)
    pos[top_idx] = np.arange(top_count)
    return top_idx,cT,Dtop,pos
```
`pair_local_search` with fractions. Need define fracs outside to avoid literal >32? 13 values okay. In function, define `fracs = (1e-4,...)` 13 scalars allowed.
Loop optimization:
```
def pair_local_search(h,n,L,Fones,deadline,max_sweeps=30,top_count=16):
    fracs = (1e-4, 3e-4, 1e-3, 3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2, 1e-1, 2e-1, 5e-1, 1.0)
    dx=2.0/n
    h=project_feasible(h,n).copy()
    cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)
    C=float(cnum.max()*dx)
    D=build_D(h,n)
    no_sweep=0
    for sweep in range(max_sweeps):
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        # rebuild every sweep to avoid drift
        cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)
        CmaxC = float(cnum.max()*dx)
        if CmaxC < C: C=CmaxC; # but h already
        else: C=CmaxC
        D=build_D(h,n)
        top_idx,cT,Dtop,pos = prepare_top_data(cnum,D,n,top_count)
        improved=False
        # maybe if cnum max C <= target? no
        for i in range(n):
            if time.time() > deadline: break
            hi_i = h[i]
            if hi_i <= 1e-12: continue # as donor no positive move? If h_i zero, no.
            Di_top = Dtop[i]
            h_i = hi_i
            for j in range(n):
                if j == i: continue
                hi = h_i if h_i < 1.0 - h[j] else 1.0 - h[j]
                if hi <= 1e-12: continue
                aT = Di_top - Dtop[j]
                idx1 = n - 1 + i - j
                p1 = pos[idx1]
                base_m = float(cT.max()) # current top max, maybe not C if outside
                # Optional: If aT all >=0 and p1? no.
                best_e = 0.0
                best_m = base_m - 1e-15
                # Evaluate fractions; skip if hi too small? all
                for f in fracs:
                    e = f * hi
                    if e <= 1e-15: continue
                    vals = cT + e * aT
                    mval = float(np.max(vals))
                    if p1 >= 0:
                        v1 = float(vals[p1]) + e*e
                        if v1 > mval: mval = v1
                    if mval < best_m:
                        best_m = mval
                        best_e = e
                if best_e <= 0: continue
                e = best_e; e2=e*e
                Afull = D[i] - D[j]
                newc = cnum + e * Afull
                newc[idx1] += e2
                idx2 = n - 1 + j - i
                newc[idx2] -= e2
                newC = float(newc.max() * dx)
                if newC < C - 1e-14:
                    h[i] = h_i - e
                    h[j] = h[j] + e
                    cnum = newc
                    C = newC
                    update_D(D, i, -e, n)
                    update_D(D, j, e, n)
                    # refresh top data
                    top_idx,cT,Dtop,pos = prepare_top_data(cnum,D,n,top_count)
                    Di_top = Dtop[i]
                    h_i = h_i - e
                    improved = True
                    # if C improves perhaps continue
            # end j
        if not improved:
            no_sweep +=1
            if no_sweep >= 2: break
        else:
            no_sweep=0
    return h,C
```

Update_D:
```
def update_D(D, s, delta, n):
    if abs(delta)<1e-15: return
    base = n-1
    # use loops r; vectorize rows?
    # first idx = base + r - s for r=0..n-1
    r = np.arange(n)
    idx1 = base + r - s
    idx2 = base + s - r
    D[r, idx1] -= delta
    D[r, idx2] -= delta
```
But if idx1 == idx2 (r=s), we subtract twice via two vector assignments, correct. Need ensure idx arrays within 0..2n-2: r-s range -(n-1)..n-1, yes. This is O(n) vectorized, much faster. D is 2D; `D[r, idx1]` with advanced indexing r array length n, idx1 array length n selects n elements; assignment works. Good.
We can precompute `r_idx`? n small. Use top-level helper maybe.
Now D build can also be vectorized? Loop slices ok.

`corr_full`: if h length not n? Ensure.
```
def corr_full(h,n,L,Fones):
    hrev = np.ascontiguousarray(h[::-1])
    Fh = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    Fv = Fones - np.fft.rfft(hrev, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(Fh * Fv, L)
    return c[:2*n-1].copy()
```
`.copy()` to avoid slice view? Not needed but okay.

`project_feasible`: if n==0 no.
```
def project_feasible(y,n):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=float)
    target = 0.5*n
    # if already feasible
    if y.size == n:
       ymin=np.min(y); ymax=np.max(y)
       if ymin >= -1e-12 and ymax <= 1+1e-12:
           s=y.sum()
           if abs(s-target) < 1e-11:
              return np.clip(y,0.0,1.0).copy()
    lo = -ymax - 1e-9
    hi = 1.0 - ymin + 1e-9
    for _ in range(60):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       if np.clip(y+mid).sum() < target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    return np.clip(y+0.5*(lo+hi))
```
If y empty? no. If ymin/ymax nan? assume no.
This returns shape n.

`full_gradient` SLSQP:
```
def full_gradient(h,n,p,dx):
    M=2*n-1
    Lg=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    cs=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(p)))
    A=cs[n:n+n]-cs[0:n]
    Fh=np.fft.rfft(h,Lg)
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,Lg)
    Fpr=np.fft.rfft(np.ascontiguousarray(p[::-1]),Lg)
    B=np.fft.irfft(Fh*Fp,Lg)[n-1:2*n-1]
    T=np.fft.irfft(Fh*Fpr,Lg)[n-1:2*n-1]
    return dx*(A-B-T)
```
This uses next_pow2 each gradient; can precompute in state OBJ_LG. Set state. But okay. To optimize, set `OBJ_LG` and use. State function:
```
OBJ_LG=0
def set_obj_state(n,L,Fones,tau,deadline):
  global ...
  OBJ_LG=next_pow2(3*n-2)
```

```
def _smooth_grad(x):
   if time.time()>OBJ_DEADLINE: raise RuntimeError
   c=corr_full(x,OBJ_N,OBJ_L,OBJ_FONES)*(2.0/OBJ_N)
   tau=OBJ_TAU
   if tau <= 1e-14:
      idx=int(np.argmax(c)); p=np.zeros(2*OBJ_N-1); p[idx]=1.0
   else:
      z=c/tau? use stable
      idxs? We need full dense p. Sm = c - c.max(); if tau small; exp.
      e=np.exp((c - c.max())/tau)
      s=e.sum(); if s<=0: p top one else p=e/s
   return full_gradient(x,OBJ_N,p,2.0/OBJ_N,OBJ_LG)
```

`_smooth_obj`:
```
def _smooth_obj(x):
  if time.time()>OBJ_DEADLINE: raise
  c=corr_full(x,OBJ_N,OBJ_L,OBJ_FONES)*(2.0/OBJ_N)
  if OBJ_TAU <=1e-14: return float(c.max())
  sm=c - c.max()
  val = float(c.max()+ np.log(np.exp(sm/OBJ_TAU).sum())/OBJ_TAU)
  if not np.isfinite(val): return float(c.max())
  return val
```
Wait logsumexp formula: If sm<=0, LSE = max + log(sum exp(sm/tau))/tau. Yes.
Gradient of this objective? Using p = exp(c/tau)/sum = exp((c-max)/tau)/sum. dF/dh = Σ p ∂c. Good. Note if F = max + logsum/tau, gradient same. Full gradient with p and dx. Good.

```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    total=max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline=t0+total-6.0
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
       h=np.asarray(initial_h_values,dtype=float).copy()
       n=len(h)
    except Exception:
       n=480
       x=np.linspace(0,2,n)
       h=0.5+0.5*np.sin(4*np.pi*x*3.0+0.37)
       h=project_feasible(h,n)
    if n < 2: return ...? no
    if n % 2 != 0:
       h=resample_h(h,n,n+1); n+=1
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    L,Fones=make_fft_plan(n)
    best_c=evaluate_h(h,n,L,Fones); best_h=h.copy()
    def? no closures. Use inline evaluate.

    # ADAM phase
    p1=t0+0.15*total
    if time.time()<p1:
       for tau in (0.03,0.012,0.006):
          if time.time()>p1-2: break
          hs,cs=adam_optimize(best_h,n,L,Fones,min(p1,deadline+?) , tau, top_k=16, lr=0.05, max_iters=8000)
          if cs<best_c: best_h,best_c=hs,cs
    # SLSQP phase
    p2=t0+0.55*total
    taus=(0.05,0.025,0.012,0.006,0.003)
    for tau in taus:
       if time.time()>p2-30: break
       # maybe maxiter based on remaining
       htry=slsqp_smooth(best_h,n,L,Fones,tau,180,p2)
       ce=evaluate_h(htry,n,L,Fones)
       if ce<best_c: best_h,htry? best_c=ce; best_h=htry.copy()
    # local phase 1
    p3=t0+0.78*total
    hs,cs=pair_local_search(best_h,n,L,Fones,p3,max_sweeps=40,top_count=16)
    if cs<best_c: ...
    # final SLSQP small from best
    if time.time()<deadline-80:
       for tau in (0.0015,0.0008,0.0004):
          if time.time()>deadline-40: break
          htry=slsqp_smooth(best_h,n,L,Fones,tau,120,deadline-20)
          ce=evaluate...
          if ce<best_c: ...
    # multi-start if time
    if time.time()<deadline-120:
       for scale in (0.005,0.01,0.02,0.04):
          if time.time()>deadline-60: break
          htry=project_feasible(best_h + scale*rng.randn(n), n)
          # if perturbed much worse, still SLSQP maybe
          htry=slsqp_smooth(htry,n,L,Fones,0.006,120,deadline-30)
          ce=evaluate...
          if ce<best_c: ...
    # final local
    hs,cs=pair_local_search(best_h,n,L,Fones,deadline-2,max_sweeps=20,top_count=12)
    if cs<best_c: ...
    # final project and eval
    best_h=project_feasible(best_h,n)
    best_c=evaluate_h(best_h,n,L,Fones)
    # fix sum
    best_h=normalize_sum(best_h,n)
    best_c=evaluate_h(best_h,n,L,Fones)
    return best_h,best_c,n
```

`normalize_sum`:
```
def normalize_sum(h,n):
    target=0.5*n
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    for _ in range(8):
        diff=target-h.sum()
        if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
        if diff>0:
            idx=int(np.argmax(1.0-h))
        else:
            idx=int(np.argmin(h))
        h[idx]=np.clip(h[idx]+diff,0,1)
    return h
```
If all at bounds diff impossible. Good. This may make sum not exact due floating; but repeated.

```
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

OBJ_N=OBJ_L=OBJ_LG=0
OBJ_FONES=None
OBJ_TAU=0.01
OBJ_DL=0.0

def next_pow2(m): ...
def project_feasible(y,n): ...
def make_fft_plan(n): ...
def corr_full(h,n,L,Fones): ...

def set_obj_state(...): ...
def c_obj(x): ...
def c_grad(x): ...
```

`c_obj`: stable LSE:
```
def c_obj(x):
    if time.time() > OBJ_DL:
        raise RuntimeError
    c = corr_full(x, OBJ_N, OBJ_L, OBJ_FONES) * (2.0 / OBJ_N)
    m = float(c.max())
    if OBJ_TAU <= 1e-14:
        return m
    e = np.exp((c - m) / OBJ_TAU)
    return float(m + np.log(e.sum()) / OBJ_TAU)
```
If e.sum() underflows to 0? Since one term exp(0)=1, sum>=1. Good.
`c_grad`:
```
def c_grad(x):
    if time.time() > OBJ_DL: raise RuntimeError
    c = corr_full(...) * (2.0/OBJ_N)
    tau=OBJ_TAU
    M=2*OBJ_N-1
    if tau <= 1e-14:
       p=np.zeros(M); p[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else:
       e=np.exp((c - float(c.max()))/tau)
       p=e/e.sum()
    return full_grad(x, OBJ_N, p, 2.0/OBJ_N, OBJ_LG)
```
Potential `c.max()` twice; store m.

```
A=cs[k+1?]? Need A_i = cs[i+n]-cs[i]. So A=cs[n:n+n]-cs[0:n]
```

`slsqp`:
```
def slsqp(h,n,L,Fones,tau,maxiter,dl):
    set_obj_state(n,L,Fones,tau,dl)
    x=project_feasible(h,n).copy()
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    cons=({'type':'eq','fun':c_con,'jac':c_jac},)
    try:
        r=minimize(c_obj,x,jac=c_grad,method='SLSQP',bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12,'disp':0})
        x=r.x
    except Exception:
        x=h
    return project_feasible(x,n)
```
If timeout raise, `x=h` initial. Good.

`build_D`:
```
def build_D(h,n):
    D=np.zeros((n,2*n-1))
    f=1.0-h[::-1]
    for r in range(n):
        D[r,r:r+n]+=f
        D[r,n-1-r:2*n-1-r]-=h
    return D
```
`upd_D`:
```
def upd_D(D,s,delta,n):
    if abs(delta)<1e-15: return
    r=np.arange(n)
    b=n-1
    D[r,b+r-s]-=delta
    D[r,b+s-r]-=delta
```
Need ensure advanced indexing works when arrays same shape. Yes. But `D[r, b+r-s]` with r int array and idx int array selects elements; assignment to D with advanced indexing and another assignment later? If both idx arrays same, first assignment updates selected; second assignment uses original D values? It's separate statement, uses current D and sets selected by subtract delta again. Good. If idx1==idx2, subtract twice. If not, okay.
But advanced indexing with duplicate indices? For a given r, idx1 and idx2 could duplicate only when r=s in separate arrays; not within one array? idx1 array may have unique? r-s yields unique over r, so no duplicates. idx2 unique. Good.

`top_data`:
```
def top_data(c,D,n,k):
    M=2*n-1
    t=M if k>M else k
    idx=np.argsort(c)[-t:]
    cT=c[idx].copy()
    Dtop=D[:,idx].copy()
    pos=np.full(M,-1,np.int32)
    pos[idx]=np.arange(t)
    return idx,cT,Dtop,pos,t
```

`pair_search`:
```
PAIR_FRACS=(...)
def pair_search(h,n,L,Fones,dl,max_sw,k):
    dx=2.0/n
    h=project_feasible(h,n).copy()
    best_h=h.copy()
    cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)
    best_C=eval_number? float(cnum.max()*dx)
    C=best_C
    for sw in range(max_sw):
        if time.time()>dl: break
        cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)
        Cact=float(cnum.max()*dx)
        if Cact < best_C - 1e-12:
            best_C=Cact; best_h=h.copy()
        elif Cact > best_C + 1e-9:
            h=best_h.copy(); cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones); C=best_C
        else:
            C=Cact
        D=build_D(h,n)
        top_idx,cT,Dtop,pos,kk=top_data(cnum,D,n,k)
        improved=False
        rvec=np.arange(n)
        for i in range(n):
            if time.time()>dl: break
            hi=h[i]
            if hi <= 1e-12: continue
            Di=Dtop[i]
            for j in range(n):
                if i==j: continue
                hj=h[j]
                cap=1.0-hj
                move=hi if hi<cap else cap
                if move <= 1e-12: continue
                aT=Di - Dtop[j]
                if float(np.min(aT)) >= -1e-11: continue
                base=float(cT.max())
                idx1=n-1+i-j; idx2=n-1+j-i
                p1=pos[idx1]; p2=pos[idx2]
                best_e=0.0; bm=base - 1e-13
                for f in PAIR_FRACS:
                    e=f*move
                    if e <= 1e-14: continue
                    vals=cT + e*aT
                    mv=float(np.max(vals))
                    if p1>=0:
                        tt=float(vals[p1]) + e*e
                        if tt>mv: mv=tt
                    if p2>=0:
                        tt=float(vals[p2]) + e*e
                        if tt>mv: mv=tt
                    if mv < bm:
                        bm=mv; best_e=e
                if best_e <= 0: continue
                e=best_e
                newc=cnum + e*(D[j]-D[i])
                e2=e*e
                newc[idx1]+=e2
                newc[idx2]+=e2
                newc[n-1]-=2.0*e2
                nc=float(newc.max()*dx)
                if nc < C - 1e-13:
                    h[i]-=e; h[j]+=e
                    cnum=newc
                    C=nc
                    upd_D(D,i,-e,n); upd_D(D,j,e,n)
                    top_idx,cT,Dtop,pos,kk=top_data(cnum,D,n,k)
                    Di=Dtop[i]
                    improved=True
        if not improved: break
    # final verify
    cnum=corr_full(h,n,L,Fones)
    C=float(cnum.max()*dx)
    if C < best_C:
        best_C=C; best_h=h.copy()
    # restore best if drift? We already had best.
    return best_h,best_C
```
Need after update, `h_i` variable `hi` should update; `h` changed. In inner loop, `hi` local old; after update, for next j, `hi` should be new h[i]. We assign? We didn't. Add after update: `hi = h[i]`. Also `Di` refreshed. Good.
In inner, if h[j] changes from previous moves, `hj` local from h before? We fetch each j. After move, next j fetches new.

```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kw):
    t0=time.time(); total=float(budget_s); dl=t0+total-6
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
       h=np.asarray(initial_h_values,dtype=float).copy(); n=len(h)
    except Exception:
       n=480
       x=np.linspace(0,2,n)
       h=0.5+0.5*np.sin(6*np.pi*x*3.0+0.4)
       h=project_feasible(h,n)
    if n%2:
       h=resample(h,n,n+1); n+=1
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    L,Fones=make_fft_plan(n)
    best_c=eval_h(h,n,L,Fones); best_h=h.copy()
    p2=t0+0.45*total
    for tau in (0.05,0.025,0.012,0.006,0.003):
       if time.time()>p2-20: break
       sub=min(p2, time.time()+80)
       ht=slsqp(best_h,n,L,Fones,tau,90,sub)
       ct=eval_h(ht,n,L,Fones)
       if ct<best_c: best_h,best_c=ht,ct
    p3=t0+0.75*total
    ht,ct=pair_search(best_h,n,L,Fones,p3,40,16)
    if ct<best_c: best_h,best_c=ht,ct
    if time.time()<dl-70:
       for tau in (0.0015,0.0008):
          if time.time()>dl-40: break
          sub=min(dl-20, time.time()+50)
          ht=slsqp(best_h,n,L,Fones,tau,70,sub)
          ct=eval_h(ht,n,L,Fones)
          if ct<best_c: best_h,best_c=ht,ct
    if time.time()<dl-120:
       for sc in (0.004,0.008,0.015,0.03):
          if time.time()>dl-60: break
          ht=project_feasible(best_h+sc*rng.randn(n),n)
          sub=min(dl-40, time.time()+60)
          ht=slsqp(ht,n,L,Fones,0.006,80,sub)
          ct=eval_h(ht,n,L,Fones)

    ht,ct=pair_search(best_h,n,L,Fones,dl-2,25,12)
    if ct<best_c: best_h,best_c=ht,ct
    best_h=norm_sum(best_h,n)
    best_c=eval_h(best_h,n,L,Fones)
    return best_h,best_c,n
```

```python
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

OBJ_N = 0
OBJ_L = 0
OBJ_LG = 0
OBJ_FONES = None
OBJ_TAU = 0.01
OBJ_DL = 0.0

PAIR_FRACS = (1e-4, 3e-4, 1e-3, 3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2, 1e-1, 2e-1, 5e-1, 1.0)


def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def project_feasible(y, n):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=float)
    target = 0.5 * n
    if y.size == n:
        ymin = float(np.min(y))
        ymax = float(np.max(y))
        if ymin >= -1e-12 and ymax <= 1.0 + 1e-12:
            s = float(y.sum())
            if abs(s - target) < 1e-11:
                return np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0).copy()
    else:
        ymin = float(np.min(y))
        ymax = float(np.max(y))
    lo = -ymax - 1e-9
    hi = 1.0 - ymin + 1e-9
    for _ in range(55):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        if float(np.clip(y + mid).sum()) < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    return np.clip(y + 0.5 * (lo + hi))


def make_fft_plan(n):
    L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Fones = np.fft.rfft(np.ones(n), L)
    return L, Fones


def corr_full(h, n, L, Fones):
    hrev = np.ascontiguousarray(h[::-1])
    Fh = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    Fv = Fones - np.fft.rfft(hrev, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(Fh * Fv, L)
    return np.real(c[:2 * n - 1])


def eval_h(h, n, L, Fones):
    return float(np.max(corr_full(h, n, L, Fones)) * (2.0 / n))


def full_grad(h, n, p, dx, Lg):
    cs = np.empty(len(p) + 1, dtype=float)
    cs[0] = 0.0
    cs[1:] = np.cumsum(p)
    A = cs[n:n + n] - cs[0:n]
    Fh = np.fft.rfft(h, Lg)
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, Lg)
    Fpr = np.fft.rfft(np.ascontiguousarray(p[::-1]), Lg)
    B = np.fft.irfft(Fh * Fp, Lg)[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    T = np.fft.irfft(Fh * Fpr, Lg)[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    return dx * (A - B - T)


def set_obj_state(n, L, Fones, tau, dl):
    global OBJ_N, OBJ_L, OBJ_LG, OBJ_FONES, OBJ_TAU, OBJ_DL
    OBJ_N = n
    OBJ_L = L
    OBJ_LG = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    OBJ_FONES = Fones
    OBJ_TAU = tau
    OBJ_DL = dl


def c_obj(x):
    if time.time() > OBJ_DL:
        raise RuntimeError("timeout")
    c = corr_full(x, OBJ_N, OBJ_L, OBJ_FONES) * (2.0 / OBJ_N)
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return 1e100
    if OBJ_TAU <= 1e-14:
        return m
    e = np.exp((c - m) / OBJ_TAU)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return m
    return float(m + np.log(s) / OBJ_TAU)


def c_grad(x):
    if time.time() > OBJ_DL:
        raise RuntimeError("timeout")
    c = corr_full(x, OBJ_N, OBJ_L, OBJ_FONES) * (2.0 / OBJ_N)
    m = float(c.max())
    M = 2 * OBJ_N - 1
    if OBJ_TAU <= 1e-14:
        p = np.zeros(M, dtype=float)
        p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        e = np.exp((c - m) / OBJ_TAU)
        s = float(e.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            p = np.zeros(M, dtype=float)
            p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            p = e / s
    return full_grad(x, OBJ_N, p, 2.0 / OBJ_N, OBJ_LG)


def c_con(x):
    if time.time() > OBJ_DL:
        raise RuntimeError("timeout")
    return float(x.sum()) - 0.5 * OBJ_N


def c_jac(x):
    if time.time() > OBJ_DL:
        raise RuntimeError("timeout")
    return np.ones(OBJ_N, dtype=float)


def slsqp_smooth(h, n, L, Fones, tau, maxiter, dl):
    set_obj_state(n, L, Fones, tau, dl)
    x0 = project_feasible(h, n).copy()
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    cons = ({
        "type": "eq",
        "fun": c_con,
        "jac": c_jac
    },)
    try:
        r = minimize(c_obj, x0, jac=c_grad, method="SLSQP",
                     bounds=bounds, constraints=cons,
                     options={"maxiter": maxiter, "ftol": 1e-13, "disp": 0})
        x = r.x
    except Exception:
        x = x0
    return project_feasible(x, n)


def build_D(h, n):
    D = np.zeros((n, 2 * n - 1), dtype=float)
    f = 1.0 - h[::-1]
    for r in range(n):
        D[r, r:r + n] += f
        D[r, n - 1 - r:2 * n - 1 - r] -= h
    return D


def upd_D(D, s, delta, n):
    if abs(delta) < 1e-15:
        return
    r = np.arange(n)
    b = n - 1
    D[r, b + r - s] -= delta
    D[r, b + s - r] -= delta


def top_data(cnum, D, n, k):
    M = 2 * n - 1
    if k > M:
        k = M
    idx = np.argsort(cnum)[-k:]
    cT = cnum[idx].copy()
    Dtop = D[:, idx].copy()
    pos = np.full(M, -1, dtype=np.int32)
    pos[idx] = np.arange(k, dtype=np.int32)
    return idx, cT, Dtop, pos, k


def pair_search(h, n, L, Fones, dl, max_sweeps, top_count):
    dx = 2.0 / n
    h = project_feasible(h, n).copy()
    cnum = corr_full(h, n, L, Fones)
    best_C = float(cnum.max() * dx)
    best_h = h.copy()
    C = best_C

    for _ in range(max_sweeps):
        if time.time() > dl:
            break
        cnum = corr_full(h, n, L, Fones)
        Cact = float(cnum.max() * dx)
        if Cact < best_C - 1e-12:
            best_C = Cact
            best_h = h.copy()
            C = Cact
        elif Cact > best_C + 1e-9:
            h = best_h.copy()
            cnum = corr_full(h, n, L, Fones)
            C = best_C
        else:
            C = Cact

        D = build_D(h, n)
        top_idx, cT, Dtop, pos, kk = top_data(cnum, D, n, top_count)
        improved = False
        p0 = pos[n - 1]

        for i in range(n):
            if time.time() > dl:
                break
            hi = float(h[i])
            if hi <= 1e-12:
                continue
            Di = Dtop[i]
            for j in range(n):
                if i == j:
                    continue
                hj = float(h[j])
                cap = 1.0 - hj
                move = hi if hi < cap else cap
                if move <= 1e-12:
                    continue

                aT = Di - Dtop[j]
                if float(np.min(aT)) >= -1e-11:
                    continue

                base = float(cT.max())
                idx1 = n - 1 + i - j
                idx2 = n - 1 + j - i
                p1 = pos[idx1]
                p2 = pos[idx2]

                best_e = 0.0
                bm = base - 1e-13
                for f in PAIR_FRACS:
                    e = f * move
                    if e <= 1e-14:
                        continue
                    e2 = e * e
                    vals = cT + e * aT
                    if p0 >= 0:
                        vals[p0] -= e2
                    if p1 >= 0:
                        vals[p1] += e2
                    if p2 >= 0:
                        vals[p2] += e2
                    mv = float(np.max(vals))
                    if mv < bm:
                        bm = mv
                        best_e = e

                if best_e <= 0.0:
                    continue

                e = best_e
                e2 = e * e
                A = D[j] - D[i]
                newc = cnum + e * A
                newc[idx1] += e2
                newc[idx2] += e2
                newc[n - 1] -= 2.0 * e2
                nc = float(np.max(newc) * dx)

                if nc < C - 1e-13:
                    h[i] -= e
                    h[j] += e
                    cnum = newc
                    C = nc
                    upd_D(D, i, -e, n)
                    upd_D(D, j, e, n)
                    top_idx, cT, Dtop, pos, kk = top_data(cnum, D, n, top_count)
                    p0 = pos[n - 1]
                    Di = Dtop[i]
                    hi = float(h[i])
                    improved = True

        if not improved:
            break

    cnum = corr_full(h, n, L, Fones)
    Cfin = float(cnum.max() * dx)
    if Cfin < best_C:
        best_C = Cfin
        best_h = h.copy()
    return best_h, best_C


def norm_sum(h, n):
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    for _ in range(10):
        diff = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmin(h))
        h[idx] = float(np.clip(h[idx] + diff, 0.0, 1.0))
    return h


def resample_h(h0, n0, n1):
    if n1 == n0:
        return project_feasible(h0, n0)
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n0)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n1)
    return project_feasible(np.interp(x1, x0, h0), n1)


def fallback_initial(n, seed):
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n)
    h = 0.5 + 0.25 * np.sin(6.0 * np.pi * x) + 0.25 * np.cos(10.0 * np.pi * x + 0.7)
    h = project_feasible(h + 0.02 * rng.randn(n), n)
    return h


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    total = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    dl = t0 + total - 6.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    try:
        h = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n = len(h)
    except Exception:
        n = 480
        h = fallback_initial(n, seed)

    if n < 2:
        n = 2
        h = np.array([0.5, 0.5])
    if n % 2 != 0:
        h = resample_h(h, n, n + 1)
        n += 1

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    L, Fones = make_fft_plan(n)
    best_h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_c = eval_h(best_h, n, L, Fones)

    # Smooth constrained SLSQP stages, analytic gradient.
    p2 = t0 + 0.45 * total
    for tau in (0.05, 0.025, 0.012, 0.006, 0.003):
        if time.time() > p2 - 25.0:
            break
        sub_dl = min(p2, time.time() + 80.0)
        ht = slsqp_smooth(best_h, n, L, Fones, tau, 90, sub_dl)
        ct = eval_h(ht, n, L, Fones)
        if ct < best_c:
            best_h = project_feasible(ht, n)
            best_c = ct

    # Exact continuous two-coordinate local search.
    p3 = t0 + 0.78 * total
    ht, ct = pair_search(best_h, n, L, Fones, min(p3, dl - 30.0), 45, 16)
    if ct < best_c:
        best_h = project_feasible(ht, n)
        best_c = ct

    # Final high-resolution smoothing.
    if time.time() < dl - 70.0:
        for tau in (0.0015, 0.0008):
            if time.time() > dl - 45.0:
                break
            sub_dl = min(dl - 20.0, time.time() + 55.0)
            ht = slsqp_smooth(best_h, n, L, Fones, tau, 70, sub_dl)
            ct = eval_h(ht, n, L, Fones)
            if ct < best_c:
                best_h = project_feasible(ht, n)
                best_c = ct

    # Careful multistart perturbation, only if enough time remains.
    if time.time() < dl - 110.0:
        for sc in (0.008, 0.02):
            if time.time() > dl - 50.0:
                break
            ht = project_feasible(best_h + sc * rng.randn(n), n)
            sub_dl = min(dl - 35.0, time.time() + 55.0)
            ht = slsqp_smooth(ht, n, L, Fones, 0.006, 80, sub_dl)
            ct = eval_h(ht, n, L, Fones)
            if ct < best_c:
                best_h = project_feasible(ht, n)
                best_c = ct

    # Final local polish until time expires.
    ht, ct = pair_search(best_h, n, L, Fones, dl - 2.0, 30, 12)
    if ct < best_c:
        best_h = project_feasible(ht, n)
        best_c = ct

    best_h = norm_sum(best_h, n)
    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c = eval_h(best_h, n, L, Fones)
    return best_h, best_c, n
```
